Summary 189 SummaryA Study of Science, Technology and Innovation (STI) Policy Diagnosis FrameworkㆍProject Leader: Jungwoo LeeㆍParticipants: Kibeom Park ․ Seogwon Hwang ․ T aejeong Ha ․ Doohyun Ahn ․ Hyeonchae Yang ․ Hyeok Lee ․ Eunji Mok ․ Reeda Ha ․ Jiseon Kim ․ Seunghwan Oh ․ Y eongjun YeoChapter 1 Introduction□(Background) While the National R&D Program continues to expand as a policy instrument to promote national science and technology capabilities and accomplish economic and social goals, it has failed to find clear-cut answ ers regarding the achievements and evaluations of science and technology policies.◦Besides this, negative perceptions and evaluations about R&D investments vs. achievements have recently increased.- Research productivity of government-funded research institutes for the past five years is reported at about 2%-3%, which is lower than that of the US (about 10%). Patents applied for by government-funded research institutes account for a low share at 38.9%. ◦In order to appease voices raising such issues persistently, it is necessary to make a comprehensive diagnosis on science and technology policies and improve their efficiency and effectiveness based on the diagnosis.

===== PDF_PAGE 224 =====
190 과학기술혁신 정책진단 프레임워크 구축◦Policy evaluations in the field of scientific and technological innovation so far have revolved around national R&D projects and institutions.- For program-level evaluations, a relatively well-structured framework is in place, including a preliminary feasibility study in the planning phase, interim evaluations during the implementation phase, and performance evaluation in the final phase.[Summary Figure 1] National R&D Program Evaluation SystemType of evaluationEvaluation target & procedures

Evaluation result feedback-Selection of a project for preliminary feasibility study (technology evaluation): Select a project for preliminary feasibility study -Interim: Spending is restructured by the depart-ment or agency concerned. Used to adjust budget allocations. Rewards or other incentives are awarded to institutions or researchers.-Final: Used as key data for tracking evaluations and selected as subject to follow-up checks, in-centives including being used for the planning and management of other projects as well as government rewards-Tracking: Incentives are awarded, including being used for the planning and management of other projects as well as government rewards.-Specific: Reflected in the budget adjustment for the project concerned or program restructuringOperational budget for the institution, performance-based pay, rewards, etc.▶
◦It is difficult to measure the attainment of the ultimate goals of a scientific and technological innovation policy based only on individual program evaluations. - Even for a successful program, it is necessary to clearly establish what the program’s ultimate goals are and where it stands within the big picture of the national scientific and technological policy.

===== PDF_PAGE 225 =====
Summary 191 □(Purpose) This study intends to go beyond the existing ‘program’-oriented evaluations and propose an analysis framew ork to ‘diagnose’ ‘policy’ as a higher-level concept.◦When we talk about ‘policy’ or ‘policies’ in this study, we do not mean ‘programs’. We are referring to policies that are higher in level (long-/mid-term plans).- National policy agenda or key basic plans for science and technology can be said to be the starting point of individual programs; therefore, this study looks into the adequacy of the planning process and the efficiency of the implementation process as well as the extent of goal attainment.
National policy agenda for science and technologyBasic plan for science and technologyComprehensive plan for each areaDetailed plan for each areaImplement the program and improve institutional systemsScope of the processes requiring a diagnosis framework
[Summary Figure 2] Scope of analysis for this study
◦ This study intends to look into what areas ‘diagnosis’, a very widely used word, covers in terms of policy evaluation as seen by the science of public administration as well as to build a policy diagnosis framework.

===== PDF_PAGE 226 =====
192 과학기술혁신 정책진단 프레임워크 구축□Main contents of this study◦ (Chapter 2) looks into policy evaluation theories in the science of public administration and identifies the areas that can be covered by a concept called ‘policy diagnosis’.◦(Chapter 3) In order to set the basic structure of a policy diagnosis framework, this chapter analyzes a broad range of other studies that have been previously conducted on long-/mid-term plans for scientific and technological innovation and identifies their significance and limitations.◦(Chapter 4) develops and designs a policy (long-/mid-term plans) diagnosis framework in the field of scientific and technological innovation.◦(Chapter 5) conducts a policy diagnosis pilot study on the government’s Third Master Plan for the Promotion of Basic Research (2013-2017) to assess the applicability and validity of the framework.◦(Chapter 6) puts together research results, draws diverse implications, and sets forth limitations in research and future challenges.Chapter 2 Review of Policy Evaluation Theories from the Perspective of Public Administration□Definition of policy evaluation◦ The science of public administration defines policy evaluation as the “objective, systematic and empirical examination of the effects ongoing policies have on their targets in terms of the goals they are meant to achieve.- The OECD Development Assistance Committee (DAC) defines policy evaluation as “the systematic and objective assessment of an ongoing or completed project, program or policy, its design, implementation and results to correct what went wrong”.

===== PDF_PAGE 227 =====
Summary 193 - Policy evaluation can be defined as the systematic assessment and review of policy formulation, execution process, and results/effects.[Summary Figure 4] Policy process
Causes of a policyFeedbackPolicyEffects of a policyJudgm entPolicy form ulationPolicy executionPolicy evaluation. Process of formulating a policy. Process of setting policy agenda, developing policy alternatives (policy analysis) and legalizing them (policy decision). Legislation process, long-/mid-term planning process . Reviewed against intended goals. In a broader sense, it includes not only the measurements of the effects of a policy but also a review on its efficiency and the causes of its success or failure.. All activities involved in executing a policy. Included are such activities as acquiring resources as well as planning, organizing and implementing programs.◦As a policy evaluation system, the Korean government is currently enforcing the Public Service Evaluation System, pursuant to the Framework Act on Public Service Evaluation.- The Office for Government Policy Coordination (OGPC) and the Government Performance Evaluation Committee take the lead in evaluating planning or execution processes and results of polices or programs carried out by central government agencies, local governments or public institutions.<Summary Table 1> Current state and types of public services evaluationTarget organizationEvaluation classificationEvaluation areaEvaluated byEvaluation type/evaluation levelCentral governm ent agenciesSpecific evaluationNational policy agenda, regulatory innovation, government innovation, policy com m unicationOGPC, M inistry of the Interior and Safety (M OIS), M inistry of Culture, Sports and Tourism (M CST) Policy and program evaluation/institutional evaluationSelf-evaluationKey policiesOGPCPolicy evaluation, program evaluation

□Types of policy evaluation◦Policy evaluation is classified into summative evaluation, process evaluation, and meta evaluation (higher-level evaluation).-Summative evaluation: includes outcome/performance/impact evaluations. It evaluates completed policies for efficiency, effectiveness and fairness.- Process evaluation: assesses plans, procedures, resources and activities

 Source: Youngsoo Ryu et al. (2017)□Policy evaluation methods◦A policy evaluation method is described as an approach to designing policy evaluation. It is also a well-defined data analysis plan to produce information that fits the purpose. In this respect, policy evaluation design approaches refer to ways of developing methodological strategies, based on evaluation theories, to design a policy evaluation study.- A true experimental evaluation design allows randomized experimental and control groups to be used and policy interventions to be adjusted.

196 과학기술혁신 정책진단 프레임워크 구축- A quasi-experimental evaluation design is used in situations where it is not possible to ensure the random assignment of groups or apply identical or comparable control groups.- A non-experimental design is used when it is not easy to manipulate policy interventions or there are limits in terms of recognizing changes after or due to a policy intervention. It can also be used when the random selection of groups cannot be guaranteed or pre-test control groups cannot be formed. <Summary Table 3> Comparison of policy evaluation design approachesExperimental design approachNon-experimental/pre-experimental design approachTrue experimental evaluationQuasi-experimental evaluationEvaluation theoryPrerequisitePrerequisiteNot relevantProgram modelPrerequisitePrerequisiteNot relevant Evaluation targetLimitedLimitedExtensive/limitedEvaluation techniqueM ostly quantitative analysisQualitative analysis is possibleMostly quantitative analysisQualitative analysis is possibleMostly qualitative analysisQuantitative analysis is possibleThreats to validityInternal validity: highExternal validity: medium/lowInternal validity: mediumExternal validity: highInternal validity: lowExternal validity: medium/lowLimitations in applying the method to policy evaluationIn real life, there are limits to applying it as manipulations of policy interventions cannot be excluded.Applicable to actual evaluationsApplicable to actual evaluationsOverall commentsIt can be used after constraints in real life are overcome.Highly applicable to real-life usesNeed to be applied in parallel with an experimental design approach Note 1) Internal validity: degree of confidence that the relationship being tested is not influenced by other factors or variables Note 2) External validity: degree of similarity to actual situations (how generalizable the findings are)□Discussions on policy evaluation targets in the field of science and technology innovation◦The Act on the Performance Evaluation and Management of National Research and Development Projects defines policy evaluation as “evaluation of research and development activities in the field of science and technology promoted by the government, which is classified into program evaluation, project evaluation, and government-sponsored institution evaluation.”

===== PDF_PAGE 231 =====
Summary 197 -Evaluations conducted from the perspective of perceiving individual programs or projects as evaluation targets usually place focus on the efficiency of budgets or program or project duplication.- Policy evaluations tend to be conducted on the basis of the performance of programs on the premise that if the efficiency of all projects can be boosted, policy effects will also be enhanced.- Defining a function that goes “total sum of the achievements of individual programs = achievements of a policy” can be explained with the micro and macro paradox, and it turns out that there have been no discussions on how program evaluation results can be aggregated to determine the success or failure of a policy.- Policy-program relationships should be clarified before evaluation targets can be set at an individual program or project level, but it is not easy to take a data-driven approach to this requirement.- This means that there are needs for comprehensive and appropriate policy evaluations that enable higher-level policy adjustments.Chapter 3 Case Studies: Evaluations of Long-/Mid-Term Science and Technology Plans□Current state of long-/m id-term plan im plem entations in the field of science and technology innovation◦In order to improve the quality of life for its people as well as to continue economic growth, Korea has been systematically establishing and pushing ahead with long-/mid-term plans in the field of science and technology, which are key drivers for national competitiveness.- As of 2019, a total of 16 departments and agencies develop long-/mid-term plans of which 94.4% are based on legal grounds and 73.0% have im plem entation plans developed to realize the long-/mid-term plan concerned.

□Cases of evaluations on long-/mid-term plans for science and technology innovation◦(KISTEP, A Survey and Analysis of Long-/Mid-Term Plans for Science and Technology) This report is published every year since 2008 and investigates and analyzes all long-/mid-term plans in the field of science and technology to propose improvement opportunities that will help enhance the effectiveness of such plans.- The KISTEP conducts a survey and analysis of long-/mid-term plans for science and technology innovation, which typically span a time series of five years or longer, and identifies areas where plans need to be coordinated and connected, focusing on proposing recommendations to improve connectivity and consistency between plans.- The institute (i) analyzes connectivity between high-level master plan and

===== PDF_PAGE 234 =====
200 과학기술혁신 정책진단 프레임워크 구축detail plans, (ii) proposes recommendations to increase the effectiveness of plans and strengthen consistency between plans, (iii) checks the achievements of performance goals in each year, and (iv) performs a life-cycle analysis on the policy planning, execution, and results of long-/mid-term plans that come to completion in each year.<Summary Table 7> In-depth analysis checkpoints in A Survey and Analysis of Long-/Mid-Term Plans for Science and Technology (KISTEP)CategoryAnalysis itemsDetailsPlanning1.Validity of policy planning-Adequacy of environmental analysis in the planning phase-Appropriateness of feedback collection as well as the setup and operations of a review board2. ===== PDF_PAGE 235 =====
Summary 201 effectively because it looks only into whether the performance indicators proposed by the plans have been achieved.․(Limitation 3) It fails to clearly present how it determines whether the performance goals of a plan have been achieved, let alone its methodology selection criteria. It also does not test the feasibility of the methods and methodology used, making it necessary to increase the reliability of future evaluations.․(Limitation 4) The criteria for setting checkpoints for life-cycle analysis on the planning, execution and final results of long-/mid-term plans are rather vague, and the report also fails to provide diversity in terms of analysis items for the policy execution and final results phases.◦ (N ational Assem bly Futures Institute (N AFI), M eta Evaluation on the Long-/M id-term Planning and Execution System) The NAFI has conducted meta evaluations to enhance the effectiveness of long-/mid-term plans through in-depth analysis of key plans in each policy area as well as their current states since 2019.- It proposes a methodology to enable comprehensive evaluations across the spectrum of the long-/mid-term planning and execution system, including the content, planning process and procedures, errors or manipulations, feasibility tests, and mutual consistency of the plans.- It identifies issues to be addressed in evaluations of long-/mid-term plans and proposes a meta evaluation framework and key checkpoints based on network analysis – which is carried out on the basis of the investigations of legally-required long-/mid-term plans – as well as an expert survey and an analysis of similar cases.

Summary 203 - The NAFI’s approach is specific to systematic reviews on the content of policy at the planning phase of government-developed long-/mid-term plans in the field of science and technology innovation.- The institute presents a great diversity of policy issues (validity, objectivity, future orientation, clarity, completeness, legality, convergence, usability and engagement of a policy) and evaluation criteria by further expanding KISTEP’s approach, which is focused on mutual connectivity and consistency among scientific and technological innovation plans.- However, as the NAFI study evaluates only the content of policy planning, it also has limitations in that it fails to perform systematic evaluations on the content of a policy over its life cycle and its tangible results.◦(Youngsoo Ryu et al. (2017), An Exploratory Study on the Evaluation of R&D Policy Effectiveness) This study examines the Master Plan for the Promotion of Basic Research (2008-2012) to analyze relationships between policy and its results and measure effectiveness at a policy level.- It measures the effectiveness of the Master Plan using two factors, (i) goal attainment measurements and (ii) impact reviews, based on Vedung’s goal attainment model (2005). <Summary Table 10> A framework for evaluating the effectiveness of long-/mid-term plans by Youngsoo Ryu et al. 204 과학기술혁신 정책진단 프레임워크 구축- This study proposes the following considerations to select a target plan for analysis: (i) the R&D policy has been completed, (ii) the goals of the R&D policy are clear, (iii) the plan contains specific activities to achieve the goals of the R&D policy, and (iv) the plan specifies related programs to achieve the goals.- The Master Plan for the Promotion of Basic Research (2008-2012) fits the criteria: (i) the policy was completed in 2012, (ii) it sets out quantitative policy goals, (iii) it includes specific activities – policy projects and priority projects, and (iv) the rolling plans prepared every year clearly state R&D projects and general financial projects under each of the 18 priority projects.◦(Myonghwa Lee et al. Summary 209 Chapter 5 Science and Technology Innovation Policy Diagnosis Framework - Pilot Project□Diagnosis for the policy planning phase○Diagnostic questions were developed on the basis of the diagnosis framework, focusing on key content details that should be included in the planning phase during which a policy is designed and formulated, and a pilot diagnosis was performed for a master plan using the questions.- Questions are divided into quantitative questions – yes or no questions that allow you to make an objective diagnosis – and qualitative questions, which were developed to measure responses on the 5-point Likert scale, based on written focus group interviews and in-depth interviews with experts.- For qualitative questions, respondents added special reasons or grounds for a diagnosis.○Each of the eight items consists of at least one quantitative and two qualitative diagnostic questions.○Results of a quantitative diagnosis by the research team show that seven out of a total of 12 questions received the diagnosis of yes (‘O’), with five given no (‘X’).

Summary 213 □Diagnosis for the policy execution phase○Diagnosis for the policy execution phase is carried out for completed plans, based on their priority projects and implementation plans.○For an expert diagnosis, structured items were provided, and results were assessed on a 5-point scale.-Eight questions were designed in detail for a total of five items, and diagnosis was conducted for each year or priority project, depending on the content of the questions.- For a total of 32 detail-level questions, experts gave quantitative assessments (on a 5-point scale) and qualitative inputs (e.g., reason for an assessment), which were collected and put together for presentation in the report. - For “progress of programs/systems designed to realize priority projects”, we prepared questions for each planned year. For the rest of the diagnosis items, however, we developed questions individually for each of the four priority projects under the plan.

Extent of the progress of programs/institutional systems designed to realize priority projectsDo you think the performance of each year is good enough to realize the priority projects concerned?2. <Summary Table 15> Diagnosis items for the policy execution phase
Source: Prepared by the Research Team○Results of our diagnosis for the policy execution phase turn out to be high across the board.- The overall average is rather high, without even a single response selecting “strongly disagree” (1 point).- The averages of individual items are: 4.00 points for the appropriateness of performance to realize priority projects; 3.62, 3.80 and 3.90 for the three sub-items under connectivity with priority projects, respectively; 4.02 and 3.96 for the sub-items under the clarity of policy beneficiaries, respectively; 3.79 for the appropriateness of the policy services delivery

===== PDF_PAGE 249 =====
Summary 215 system; and 4.12 for the adequacy of reflecting changes in the policy environment.-But it is found that the items about whether the composition of implementation plans is good enough to realize priority projects and whether the policy services delivery system is appropriate recorded relatively low points.- It is also confirmed that evaluators have different perspectives on priority projects, depending on whether they are researchers or experts from the managing institutions.-Researchers gave lower points to the Nurture Leading Global Basic Research Hubs project, whereas managers assessed the Establish a Quality-Centered Evaluation System project relatively low.□Diagnosis for the policy evaluation phase○When the plan is completed, goal attainment and side-effects assessments are conducted not only for the overall aspects of the plan but also for each of its detail-level component.- (Goal attainment assessment) diagnosis on the attainment of the goals set forth in the plan.- (Side-effects assessment) This includes unexpected effects, positive or negative, other than the proposed goals. This assessment employs different diagnosis details, depending on the characteristics of an evaluation target.․As this case study is about basic research, the evaluation focused on the social and economic effects brought about by basic research policies.

===== PDF_PAGE 250 =====
216 과학기술혁신 정책진단 프레임워크 구축○A diagnostic questionnaire was used to collect and incorporate expert insights, and a comprehensive diagnosis was conducted based on their responses.- Survey questions were scored on a 5-point Likert scale, and respondents added comments if they strongly disagree to a question.- The key implementation performance details analyzed by the Research Team were provided to the experts, along with the diagnostic questions.- (Goal attainment assessment) consists of three diagnostic categories: goal attainment, goal difficulty, and performance relationship․(Goal attainment) actual achievements relative to planned policy goals․(Goal difficulty) degree of difficulty in attaining planned performance goals․(Performance relationship) extent to which an aggregated sum of outcomes is sufficient to achieve a higher-level goal․Given the hierarchy of the Master Plan for the Promotion of Basic Research, which consists of vision, performance goals, policy indicators and priority projects, a diagnosis was performed at the levels of performance goals, policy indicators and priority projects.- (Side-effects assessment) diagnosis performed from five perspectives: knowledge generation, future research, impact on policy and research activities, and extensive socioeconomic effects․(Knowledge generation) impact of basic research on improving knowledge across the spectrum of science and technology, other than the performance goals achieved․(Future research) impact of a basic research policy not only on the

===== PDF_PAGE 251 =====
Summary 217 field of basic research but also on other applications and R&D activities․(Impact on policy) impact on basic research and other plans related to science and technology․(Impact on research activities) impact on research expenses, facilities and equipment, selection of research topics, and researcher networking, which are part of the research activities actually performed by researchers․(Socioeconomic effects) extensive social and economic effects, such as revenues, exports, jobs, and employment○Although the goals set forth by the Master Plan were challenging, they were achieved, by and large, and the research environment was also improved. These effects also have lasted over the years.-(Goal attainment assessment) The goals initially proposed were challenging. While the quantitative goals were achieved to a certain extent, it is considered necessary to prepare a detailed plan in order to achieve the qualitative goals.- (Side-effects assessment) The benefits produced by the Master Plan include increased budgets, the establishment of a basic research support system, the improvement of the research environment, and talent nurturing, which have lasted over the years. However, it is still early to verify economic and social effects as they are longer-term effects.

===== PDF_PAGE 252 =====
218 과학기술혁신 정책진단 프레임워크 구축Chapter 6 Conclusion□Overall research results and implications○On the basis of an internally-developed diagnosis framework, this study developed key diagnostic questions for each phase of a master plan in the field of basic research and performed a pilot diagnosis on the plan.- Diagnostic items for the policy planning phase include ‘compliance with laws and fitness to purpose’, ‘review on similarity or duplication’, ‘concreteness of policy implementation’, ‘consistency’ and ‘validity’, among other things.-Diagnostic items for the policy execution phase consist of ‘progress of programs/systems to realize priority projects’, ‘connectivity with priority projects’, ‘clarity of policy beneficiaries’, ‘appropriateness of the policy services delivery system’ and ‘reflecting policy environment changes’.-Diagnostic item s for the policy evaluation phase are divided into ‘goal attainm ent assessment’ and ‘side-effects assessment’.○Diagnosis for each phase produced the following results:-(Policy planning) Both the researcher group and the manager group assessed ‘compliance with applicable laws and fitness to purpose’ to be highest, but gave the lowest scores to ‘engagement of the planning parties and related organizations’.-(Policy execution) Overall, the scores were high, but the ‘appropriateness of the policy services delivery system’ item was given a low score, making it necessary to have a continuous monitoring and policy execution feedback process well in place.-(Policy evaluation) The quantitative goals turned out to be largely

===== PDF_PAGE 253 =====
Summary 219 achieved, but it is considered necessary to develop detailed implementation plans to attain qualitative goals. There are also needs for post-completion monitoring and participation of experts and stakeholders in the diagnosis process.□Limitations of the study and future challenges○To make up for the limitations of dichotomous questions, it is necessary to consider the further segmentation of diagnosis (e.g., add a triangle (‘△’) to possible answers) by clarifying diagnosis content and taking into account the characteristics and levels of target policies.○In the case of qualitative diagnosis for the policy planning phase, further efforts are needed to minimize selection bias through the engagement of as diverse experts as possible in the diagnosis by excluding stakeholders and restricting the participation of people associated with the same organization.○There are limitations in making categorical diagnostic decisions and direct com parisons based only on absolute num bers, for such reasons as the absence of comparison groups, the use of the same weights, the participation of different experts for different policies, and imbalance in terms of the types of experts.- Possible to minimize bias and improve reliability by excluding the highest and lowest points to calculate averages, expanding experts participating in diagnosis and ensuring balance in terms of types of participating experts, and applying analytic hierarchy process (AHP) weights○It is expected that the diagnosis framework and questionnaires can be

===== PDF_PAGE 254 =====
220 과학기술혁신 정책진단 프레임워크 구축upgraded by improving them based on the responses and feedback from experts.- Diagnosis for the execution phase should be conducted while the target policy is being executed, but we had to allow use of a completed policy for this study because of the timing of conducting this study.- To overcome this constraint, this study presents the outcomes of priority projects in each year, implementation plans of each priority project, and results of identifying policy beneficiaries as well as changes in the policy environment and key policy responses in each year.○For diagnosis for the execution phase, it is essential to conduct continuous monitoring, to review the completeness of the implementation plan through the diagnosis, and to serve as a feedback process for the execution of the target policy.-Diagnosis for a long-/mid-term plan should be preceded by timely monitoring on its execution. It should also be performed on a monthly, quarterly and half-yearly basis by simplifying the current yearly evaluation process for this purpose. -Diagnosis for the execution phase should also be performed on implem entation plans, which typically specify vague policy indicators or priority projects in more detail.- A detail-level implementation plan is presented with performance goals or plans. This type of plan should be complete to ensure that there is nothing left out to implement a priority project and achieve policy goals.- A feedback process is important because it can be applied during a diagnosis for the execution phase to help attain policy goals.○The pilot study for the policy evaluation phase came up with the following

===== PDF_PAGE 255 =====
Summary 221 implications: There are needs for improving the concreteness and systemacity of policy goals, monitoring on inadequate outcomes after the completion of a plan, and engaging experts and stakeholders in the diagnosis process.- It is not easy to track relevant outcomes and figure out the long-/mid-term impact of a policy because performance monitoring discontinues after the completion of the planned period.․If a policy has failed to attain its goals or has fallen short of its targets during its implementation period, it is necessary to continue tracking it even after the completion of the plan.- When it comes to declarative content that fails to provide clear-cut goals, it is difficult to diagnose actuals against targets.․Targets for policy indicators are not clearly presented, giving rise to a gap between vision and priority projects in terms of the concreteness of plans.․It is necessary to review whether long-/mid-term benefits expected were identified using a systematic method, such as a logic model, in the policy formulation stage.- Side-effects assessments are strongly reflective of the specialty of a field, which means that they require different diagnosis items for different areas. Therefore, you need to involve experts in the area concerned as well as evaluations by stakeholders when designing this type of assessment.․A side-effects assessment is significant in terms of adding to the appropriateness of diagnosis items, rather than as an evaluation on the basic research policy to be assessed. Therefore, it is necessary to

===== PDF_PAGE 256 =====
222 과학기술혁신 정책진단 프레임워크 구축involve experts in the field concerned when designing a questionnaire.․When this diagnosis framework is elaborated further and applied in the future, you should consider how stakeholder involvement can be factored in to provide additional evaluation results in line with the characteristics of the field concerned.

Summary and findings· · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · ·140│Chapter 6│Conclusion· · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · 142Section 1 Overall research results and implications· · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · ·142Section 2 Limitations of the study and future challenges· · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · ·144References· · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · 147Appendix· · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · 155Summary (in English)· · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · 189Contents (in English)· · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · · 223
